Learn more about our methodology
Source: Comfy API usage (Comfy Router and partner API nodes, on Cloud and local installs), Monday-start UTC weeks from Nov 24, 2025 to Nov 22, 2026. A month is the set of weeks whose Thursday falls in it, so every week counts toward exactly one month, and only months with all their weeks in the data are shown. A generation is one call, or the number of outputs when a call asks for several images (under 1% of calls). Shares are within each output type. The chart breaks out the top 50 models over the year (by summed share); the rest are grouped as Other models. The table ranks every model by its share in the latest week. Output type comes from the model, and for new models from the call's request. Some providers don't record the model on every call (about 4% of calls, mostly Kling, Krea, Tripo, Recraft, Freepik and Vidu). Those calls use the model named in the request when there is one, and otherwise count under the provider and output type, like kling (video). Names that differ only in capitals count as one model, and models renamed from a preview name are merged with their final name. Models with fewer than 200 calls over the year are grouped as Other models. Language models and sound effects are left out.

Learn more about our methodology
Growth is the percent change in a model's generations per week from one period to the one before, from the same Comfy API usage as Top models. Week compares the latest full Monday-start UTC week with the week before. Month and Quarter compare the latest full month or quarter with the one before, where a week counts toward the month that holds its Thursday; comparing generations per week keeps 4- and 5-week months fair. Only percentages are shown, never generation counts. To keep tiny models from topping the list with huge jumps, a model is ranked only if it had at least 0.5% of its type's generations in the earlier period. Models that crossed 0.5% from below are listed as new arrivals under the chart. The type's overall change is shown for context, since a model can grow just because its whole type grew. Models renamed from a preview name are merged with their final name. Other models, language models and sound effects are left out.

Learn more about our methodology
Source: ComfyUI Desktop v2 app telemetry, one count per install per Monday-start UTC week, using its latest report that week, from Jul 6, 2026 to Nov 22, 2026. Desktop v2 only started sending hardware reports in June 2026, so there isn't a full year yet. June is left out: the early app often reported a virtual display adapter instead of the real GPU, and no VRAM. Portable, manual-install and Cloud users don't send this, so Linux and multi-GPU setups are likely undercounted. Intel and AMD integrated graphics land in under 6GB. Macs share memory between CPU and GPU, so they have their own VRAM bucket. VRAM buckets include their upper bound (an 8GB card is in 6-8GB). GPU names are cleaned up, laptop chips are kept separate, and GPUs outside the 30 most common over the whole range are grouped as Other GPUs (for Desktop v1 this includes installs with no GPU recorded).

Learn more about our methodology
Source: Comfy Cloud job records, successful jobs from Dec 1, 2025 to Nov 22, 2026. Times are medians (the typical job) and 90th percentiles (a slow job). Latency is the wait from when a job is submitted to when it starts running. Generation time is from start to finish of the run, including loading the model onto the GPU and every other step in the workflow, so it varies with steps, resolution and video length as well as the model. Each job is matched to its main diffusion model, which works for about 69% of jobs. Every model with at least 100 jobs since Dec 1, 2025 is shown. Original models are the official releases from the model makers (including Comfy's repackaged and quantized copies). LoRAs are the community fine-tunes: checkpoints and merges trained on top of the original models. When the same file is stored under several names, the most used copy is shown. Checkpoints with explicit names are left out. Every job ran on the same GPU (RTX Pro 6000). API (partner) models are left out because their timing mixes latency and generation time. Sort by Usage puts the models with the most Cloud usage first, measured as share of Cloud credits: Cloud charges credits per second of GPU time and every job ran on the same GPU, so each model's share of metered GPU time is its share of credits (metered for about 92% of the jobs shown). Sort by Fastest puts the lowest median first.

Learn more about our methodology
Source: Hugging Face downloads of the Comfy-Org organization's model repos. Up to Oct 18, 2026 the counts come from Hugging Face's publisher analytics. After that they come from the change in each repo's public download total, recorded every morning, so a period is only shown once a recording exists near its start. Weeks are Monday-start UTC weeks and the latest period runs to Nov 22, 2026. Each repo is one model family and can hold several files (precisions, text encoders, VAEs). A download is one file fetched, so a workflow that pulls three files counts three times. Models published by other organizations, or by their own labs, aren't in this data. Shares are within each output type, which is set by hand from the repo name. Text encoders and VAEs are support files other models load (the Flux Klein VAE and text encoder repos, Qwen3-VL, Gemma 4, SigCLIP).

A month is the set of weeks whose Thursday falls in it, so every week counts toward exactly one month, and only months with all their weeks in the data are shown. A generation is one call, or the number of outputs when a call asks for several images (under 1% of calls). Shares are within each output type. The chart breaks out the top `,TOPN,` models over the year (by summed share); the rest are grouped as Other models. The table ranks every model by its share in the latest `,unit,`. Output type comes from the model, and for new models from the call's request. Some providers don't record the model on every call (about 4% of calls, mostly Kling, Krea, Tripo, Recraft, Freepik and Vidu). Those calls use the model named in the request when there is one, and otherwise count under the provider and output type, like kling (video). `,tp.name,`)`]},r.name))]}):null]}),_jsx(__snippet_Methodology_1e8rbfs,{children:`Growth is the percent change in a model's generations per week from one period to the one before, from the same Comfy API usage as Top models. Week compares the latest full Monday-start UTC week with the week before. Month and Quarter compare the latest full month or quarter with the one before, where a week counts toward the month that holds its Thursday; comparing generations per week keeps 4- and 5-week months fair. Only percentages are shown, never generation counts. To keep tiny models from topping the list with huge jumps, a model is ranked only if it had at least 0.5% of its type's generations in the earlier period. Models that crossed 0.5% from below are listed as new arrivals under the chart. The type's overall change is shown for context, since a model can grow just because its whole type grew. Models renamed from a preview name are merged with their final name. Each install's hardware is its latest profile, not what it had that week, and the main GPU is the one with the most VRAM. System RAM isn't recorded, and installs with no platform land in Other. AMD's share jumps in early January 2026, which may be a change in how the app reported AMD GPUs rather than real growth.`]}),` `,`Intel and AMD integrated graphics land in under 6GB. Macs share memory between CPU and GPU, so they have their own VRAM bucket. VRAM buckets include their upper bound (an 8GB card is in 6-8GB). Comfy Cloud job records, successful jobs from `,__snippet_fmtDate_1e8rbfs(from),` to `,__snippet_fmtDate_1e8rbfs(through),`. Times are medians (the typical job) and 90th percentiles (a slow job). Latency is the wait from when a job is submitted to when it starts running. Generation time is from start to finish of the run, including loading the model onto the GPU and every other step in the workflow, so it varies with steps, resolution and video length as well as the model. Each job is matched to its main diffusion model, which works for about 69% of jobs. Every model with at least 100 jobs since `,__snippet_fmtDate_1e8rbfs(from),` is shown. Original models are the official releases from the model makers (including Comfy's repackaged and quantized copies). LoRAs are the community fine-tunes: checkpoints and merges trained on top of the original models. When the same file is stored under several names, the most used copy is shown. Checkpoints with explicit names are left out. Every job ran on the same GPU (RTX Pro 6000). API (partner) models are left out because their timing mixes latency and generation time. Sort by Usage puts the models with the most Cloud usage first, measured as share of Cloud credits: Cloud charges credits per second of GPU time and every job ran on the same GPU, so each model's share of metered GPU time is its share of credits (metered for about 92% of the jobs shown). Sort by Fastest puts the lowest median first.`]})]})};const __snippet_DownloadRankings_1e8rbfs=({data,through})=>{const TOPN=15;const OTHER=`Other Hugging Face downloads of the Comfy-Org organization's model repos. Up to Oct 18, 2026 the counts come from Hugging Face's publisher analytics. After that they come from the change in each repo's public download total, recorded every morning, so a period is only shown once a recording exists near its start. Weeks are Monday-start UTC weeks and the latest period runs to `,__snippet_fmtDate_1e8rbfs(through),`. Each repo is one model family and can hold several files (precisions, text encoders, VAEs). A download is one file fetched, so a workflow that pulls three files counts three times. Models published by other organizations, or by their own labs, aren't in this data. Shares are within each output type, which is set by hand from the repo name. Text encoders and VAEs are support files other models load (the Flux Klein VAE and text encoder repos, Qwen3-VL, Gemma 4, SigCLIP).`]})]})};const
